#2208. La Lazio Primavera vince sul campo del Cagliari: Sulejmani decisivo al 90'

Mentre al Maradona, la Lazio di Gennaro Gattuso pareggiava sul campo del Napoli, i giovani biancocelesti di Punzi hanno guadagnato 3 punti fondamentali

Mentre al Maradona, la Lazio di Gennaro Gattuso pareggiava sul campo del Napoli, i giovani biancocelesti di Punzi hanno guadagnato 3 punti fondamentali.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

La Lazio Primavera reduce dalla vittoria contro il Sassuolo nella scorsa giornata va a far visita ad un Cagliari reduce da tre vittorie consecutive considerando tutte le competizioni.

Nel primo tempo la Lazio va a più riprese vicino al vantaggio con il portiere del Cagliari che in più di un'occasione è costretto a metterci i guantoni, ma l'occasione principale è sui piedi di Ferrante, che si vede negare la gioia del gol dalla traversa. Nella ripresa è il portiere del Cagliari, Vincenzo Sarno, a prendersi la scena sfoderando diverse parate di pregevolissima fattura. A rompere la parità ci pensa, però, il subentrato Flavio Sulejmani, che al 90' grazie ad una bella girata in area buca la porta rossoblù e regala i tre punti alla Lazio.

Vittoria importantissima per i biancocelesti che con i tre punti conquistati sul campo del Cagliari si colloca al terzo posto a pari punti con il Bologna a -2 dal secondo posto e -3 dal primato con 3 punti di vantaggio sul settimo posto, che vale l'esclusione dai playoff.

#2207. Sampdoria, con l'allenatore esperto arrivano punti. E non è frutto del caso

Più o meno da una sosta all'altra, ma con un volto decisamente diverso. Perché se solo un mese fa la classifica era più che mai drammatica, e le speranze erano poche, oggi la Sampdoria si ritrova addirittura fuori dalla zona playout in Serie B; certo, non dovrebbe esser questa una notizia se si guarda il blasone del club, ma viste le ultime annate dei blucerchiati, lo è.

La risposta potrebbe sembrare facile e ovvia, c'è stato il ribaltone in panchina. E con un trainer navigato come Luca D'Angelo - nonostante la situazione di emergenza in cui versa la squadra se si guarda l'infermeria - i punti sono arrivati: sette nelle quattro gare giocate sotto la sua direzione, di cui gli ultimi sei consecutivi. Dopo il pari contro il SudTirol è arrivato il ko contro l'Ascoli, poi i successi su Virtus Entella e Carrarese, in gara che erano più o meno scontri diretti. Tre punti in più rispetto ai 4 che i blucerchiati avevano collezionato nelle prime sette partite

Sia chiaro, l'aver riportato questi numeri, evidenziando quanto accaduto, non è un buttare addosso la croce a Bernardo Corradi, l'ex trainer dei liguri; mai stato primo allenatore, si è trovato catapultato in una situazione pesante, complicata, e l'inesperienza non ha giocato a suo favore. Logicamente. È mancata forse la sana malizia, che solo i più esperti hanno. È semmai Jesper Fredberg che avrebbe dovuto far prima determinate considerazioni, anche in virtù dell'anno passato: in certe situazioni, serve il mister navigato. Ma D'Angelo è il primo della sua gestione. Come si dice però, "meglio tardi"...

#2205. What Alex Neil must learn from Millwall’s 3-0 defeat to West Brom heading into the international break

Millwall have had a terrible opening third of the 2026/27 season. The Lions suffered their seventh loss of the campaign and their second this week as West Brom were 3-0 victors at The Hawthorns on Saturday.

Alex Neil’s side did well to nullify a WBA side who were unbeaten in five before the weekend inside the first 45 minutes. However, the second half saw the South London outfit capitulate defensively as they made it 27 goals conceded this term.

Millwall will welcome the pause in Championship action for two weeks as they reset. There are certainly many lessons learned from their latest defeat that the Lions must use as fuel over the international break to come back stronger heading into the busy winter period.

This time last year, when Millwall headed into the international break, they were sat 6th in the table and just four points off the top two. Now after their second consecutive 3-0 loss, the Lions are 16th in the Championship and six points from the top eight.

Neil’s side have scored six more goals than they did this time last season. However, they have conceded seven more times. Saturday highlighted the gulf in quality and momentum between a side in real play-off contention like West Brom and a team in disarray like Millwall.

The Baggies had an xG of 2.98 whereas the South London outfit only had an xG of 0.62. The Lions also had just nine touches inside the opposition’s box and won just 48% of their ground duels.

All three goals Millwall conceded against West Brom weren’t entirely at the fault of the New Zealand international. However, it’s safe to say that the Lions won’t achieve anything this season with him as their number one.

Max Crocombe has conceded 22 goals this term out of 10 games and has a save percentage of 55.1%. He is currently averaging 2.2 goals conceded per match, and the 33-year-old is struggling to cope with the demands of the Championship.

Against WBA, the New Zealand international made an error leading to a goal and made just three saves. With Lukas Jensen reported to be nearing a return to fitness and his save percentage standing at 100% from his two games this term, Neil won’t select Crocombe again if he doesn’t have to.

One positive Millwall can take from the clash against West Brom is the return of duo Josh Coburn and Tairyk Arconte. Coburn played 54 minutes before being substituted, whilst Arconte featured for the last 10 minutes.

Both players have been crucial to the limited success Neil’s side has had this season. Coburn especially had a strong start to the term before an injury sidelined him for two months. He has won 69.2% of his aerial duels this campaign and has an xG on target of 3.14.

Arconte was also missed by the Lions. He has netted three times in nine games for Millwall in his debut Championship season. The 22-year-old has a successful dribble rate of 60% and has made 45 touches inside the opposition box.

#2201. Sabatini: “Il Napoli ha giocato le tre partite più spettacoli della stagione”

Il giornalista Sandro Sabatini, attraverso un video pubblicato sul proprio canale YouTube, ha analizzato il pareggio per 1-1 tra Napoli e Lazio: “Le tre partite più belle, spettacolari, ricche di gol, emozioni e occasioni di questa stagione sono state Inter-Napoli 3-2, Napoli-Roma 3-3 e Napoli-Lazio 1-1. Il calcio dominante, propositivo e moderno si è visto in queste tre partite del Napoli, che sono state tra le migliori di un campionato che, magari dopo la sosta, potrà riservare qualche correzione. È proprio il Napoli di Allegri ad augurarsi questa correzione, perché la classifica parla chiaro. Nonostante alcune prestazioni all’altezza della situazione, gli azzurri si ritrovano staccatissimi, intorno al decimo posto, con un divario obiettivamente incolmabile per un’eventuale lotta scudetto e quasi incolmabile persino per il quarto posto. Il calcio è così, è paradossale. Non sempre si riesce a trovare una conferma o una chiave di lettura per qualsiasi partita, a meno che non ci si voglia lasciare guidare dalle ideologie e dai commenti precostituiti, anziché dalle idee e dall’analisi. Fatta questa premessa, Napoli e Lazio hanno pareggiato 1-1 al termine di una partita molto emozionante, spettacolare e combattuta. Il primo tempo è stato maggiormente favorevole alla Lazio, che infatti lo ha concluso in vantaggio per 1-0. Nella ripresa il Napoli ha trovato il pareggio, propiziato da McTominay e Hojlund, con la complicità di Pedraza, autore dell’autogol. Gli azzurri hanno poi costruito altre occasioni, neutralizzate dal portiere. Su tutte, quella clamorosa nel duello faccia a faccia con De Bruyne. Sì, il Napoli nel secondo tempo avrebbe potuto anche vincere, ma la Lazio è una squadra solida, che merita il primo posto in classifica. Gattuso l’ha organizzata benissimo. Mi è piaciuto tantissimo il primo tempo della Lazio, ma anche la gestione degli ultimi dieci minuti, nei quali ha giocato da grande squadra. Bisogna considerare che anche la Lazio, così come il Napoli, ha dovuto fare i conti con assenze importanti. A un certo punto è stata costretta a rinunciare a Sutalo e Nuno Tavares, oltre a dover fare a meno di Zaccagni. Tre assenze pesanti, eppure la Lazio esce dal Maradona tra la soddisfazione dei propri tifosi. I sostenitori della Lazio di Gattuso festeggiano, ed è giusto che sia così. Quelli del Napoli di Allegri, invece, se la prendono con tutti, a cominciare dall’allenatore, per arrivare a quello che sembra essere diventato l’imputato numero uno. Imputato di che cosa, poi, non si capisce bene. Pensate che Noa Lang, da un certo momento in avanti, è stato fischiato per tutta la partita. Eppure Noa Lang è stato nettamente il migliore in campo del Napoli. Un giocatore vivo, sempre pronto a puntare l’uomo. Romano Floriani Mussolini, alla sua prima presenza da titolare, ha vissuto una giornata difficilissima, tanto da essere sostituito da Lazzari, perché nessuno riusciva a fermare Lang. Eppure i tifosi del Napoli lo fischiavano. Per che cosa? Per un gesto? Per una sorta di regolamento di conti? Non si capisce. Credo sia stato qualcosa che non dovrebbe trovare spazio nel giudizio su Lang. Naturalmente i tifosi possono fare quello che vogliono, ma il rendimento di un calciatore di personalità, come lo è Lang, un folle ma dotato di personalità, non dipende certo dai fischi o dagli applausi. Il rendimento di un giocatore o di una squadra non dipende dagli applausi o dai fischi dello stadio. E la dimostrazione, per certi versi, arriva proprio dalla Lazio. Mi dispiace dirlo, perché la passione dei tifosi laziali, compresi quelli che non vanno allo stadio, è fuori discussione. Però la dimostrazione che il pubblico conta fino a un certo punto arriva proprio dalla stagione della Lazio, che gioca in uno stadio quasi deserto eppure è in testa al campionato. Una squadra che sembrava destinata a lottare per non retrocedere sta invece lottando per lo scudetto. Vedremo quali saranno gli altri risultati, ma una cosa è sicura: chi, dopo queste prime giornate e le prime due soste, esclude la Lazio dalla lotta scudetto, secondo me commette un errore. È una squadra cresciuta con Gattuso, così come è cresciuto lo stesso allenatore, tornato, secondo me, alla semplicità, alla razionalità, alla precisione, al lavoro e alla solidità del suo primo staff, senza tutte le confusioni dell’esperienza in Nazionale. Quindi complimenti alla Lazio, ma per una volta complimenti anche al Napoli. Nel Napoli, Milinkovic-Savic ha parato quello che doveva parare, poi è stato impallinato da Frattesi. Sul gol del primo tempo il pallone gli passa praticamente accanto alla faccia. Per il resto è stato preciso negli interventi. Beukema un po’ meglio rispetto alle precedenti esibizioni, anche se non ci voleva molto. Rrahmani si vede che sta tirando avanti, lottando nonostante una condizione fisica complicata e dolori che potrebbero anche metterlo in difficoltà. Eppure continua a combattere con grande leadership. Rafa Marín discreto. Aveva offerto qualcosa di meglio nel corso della stagione, ma la sua resta una prestazione sufficiente. Olivera corre più di tutti, soltanto che poi sbaglia più di tutti sotto porta. È un giocatore che, se andassimo a vedere gli xG di questo campionato, probabilmente scopriremmo essere andato vicino al gol diverse volte, anche con salvataggi sulla linea. Poverino, non riesce mai a trovare la soddisfazione della rete. Nel secondo tempo ha avuto un pallone da calciare con il destro, che non è il suo piede. Era quasi un rigore, leggermente decentrato, ma lo ha mandato oltre la traversa. McTominay molto bene nel secondo tempo, così come De Bruyne, soprattutto quando i due si sono invertiti di posizione. Gilmour geometrico, didascalico e niente più. Vergara è partito molto male, ma ha concluso meglio. A me piace anche senza i gol, perché è un giocatore che, quando troverà maggiore fiducia, autostima e leggerezza mentale, potrà diventare molto più incisivo. E poi c’è Lang. È un giocatore che può dividere. Può risultare simpatico o, più probabilmente, antipatico. Può essere considerato un professionista serio, anche se non credo sia questa l’immagine che trasmette, oppure un folle, uno scapestrato, un genialoide, un artista, un rapper. Può essere considerato tutto. Ma se i tifosi del Napoli fischiano Lang per aver fatto quel gesto, significa che la competenza calcistica viene superata dall’umore, dall’invidia o da qualcosa che non ha nulla a che fare con il calcio e, se posso permettermi, nemmeno con il sostegno di cui questa squadra ha bisogno. Lo dico per i tifosi del Napoli, ma anche per quelli dell’Inter, della Roma, per quelli che l’anno scorso contestavano le proprie squadre e per quelli della Fiorentina. Se si va allo stadio concentrandosi sul fischiare un giocatore e sul trovare un capro espiatorio, come è successo anche a Lucca nell’ultimo quarto d’ora, va bene, si può fare tutto. I tifosi ne hanno il diritto, ma l’appoggio è un’altra cosa. Ed è proprio l’appoggio che i tifosi dovrebbero dare. Per me Lang è un giocatore di livello internazionale. È ovvio che non giocherà tutte le partite come questa, ma le sue qualità non si discutono. Concludendo, la Lazio è in corsa per lo scudetto. Il Napoli, se dovesse continuare a giocare così, potrebbe tornare a lottare per un posto tra le prime quattro. Ma deve continuare su questa strada”.

I Am Naples Testata Giornalistica - aut. #2200. Monaco, esultanza shock di Coulibaly: rischio squalifica, c'è un precedente

Un'esultanza destinata a far discutere in Ligue 1. Durante la sfida tra Le Mans e Monaco, valida per la decima giornata del campionato francese, Mamadou Coulibaly si è reso protagonista di un gesto controverso dopo aver portato in vantaggio la formazione monegasca nel primo tempo. Il centrocampista classe 2004, che indossa la maglia numero 28 dell'ASM, ha festeggiato la rete passandosi un dito sotto la gola, mimando il gesto del taglio della stessa. Un'esultanza che ha immediatamente richiamato alla memoria un episodio analogo, già finito al centro delle polemiche nel calcio francese.

Come sottolineato da L'Équipe, il gesto di Coulibaly ricorda quello compiuto da Paris Brunner durante una partita contro lo Strasburgo. In quell'occasione, l'attaccante era stato punito dalla commissione disciplinare della Ligue de Football Professionnel (LFP) con due giornate di squalifica per la propria esultanza. Un precedente che potrebbe pesare anche sulla posizione del calciatore del Monaco, qualora gli organi disciplinari decidessero di adottare lo stesso criterio di valutazione. Al momento non è stata comunicata alcuna sanzione nei confronti di Coulibaly, ma il gesto potrebbe essere esaminato dalla commissione competente. Il numero 28 monegasco rischia dunque di pagare a caro prezzo un'esultanza controversa, soprattutto alla luce della recente decisione presa nel caso Brunner. Resta da capire se la LFP considererà i due episodi equivalenti e applicherà una sanzione analoga.

Per la cronaca, la sfida è poi terminata 1-1 con pareggio del Le Mans in extremis.

#2198. Premier League club tracking Middlesbrough star Will Lankshear amid historic form

Middlesbrough only signed Will Lankshear in the summer, but the striker’s stunning form has brought excitement over his next steps. England boss Thomas Tuchel has eyes on the Boro star, and now, The Sun states Newcastle United are closely watching his performances.

Scouts were set to watch Lankshear in action against Wrexham on Wednesday, but injury kept him sidelined for that game and the 0-0 draw at Watford on Saturday. Kim Hellberg‘s side failed to score in both games, highlighting how much they are missing their star striker.

Lankshear has 13 goals in 13 Championship games, and 15 goals in 16 matches for the Teesside outfit across all competitions. No Boro player has started a season in such prolific form since club record goalscorer George Camsell in 1926.

Lankshear has been viewed as an exciting prospect for some time. Spurs snapped him up from the Sheffield United academy at a youth age and he really announced himself on the senior stage on loan at Oxford United, where he scored 12 goals and provided three assists in 47 games across the 2025/26 season.

Despite such success and encouragement over his future, there were eyebrows raised when Middlesbrough struck a deal worth £20m with add-ons to sign him in the summer. However, Lankshear has already justified the hefty fee.

Lankshear has scored four Championship braces on his way to the top of the goalscoring charts. He was on a run of seven goals in five games before injury hit and ruled him out of Boro’s last two matches.

The 21-year-old’s lethal finishing and trajectory have drawn early comparisons to England captain and Ballon d’Or winner Harry Kane. Lankshear is currently averaging a goal every 78 minutes for Middlesbrough, so it is little surprise to see the England national team and Premier League club Newcastle United tracking his performances.

They will not be the only ones with eyes on Lankshear, who is viewed as one of the country’s most promising young attackers.

Lankshear only moved in the summer and is under contract until 2031. It means Boro aren’t under pressure to cash in.

However, to keep him long term, if Middlesbrough are to have a chance of fending off top interest, they have to ensure their trajectory matches that of Lankshear. The only way to do so is by winning promotion to the Premier League this season.

If not, there will be no shortage of clubs ready to spend big on bringing him in. Newcastle United are the only concretely reported admirer at present, but he is sure to have alerted more sides.

Middlesbrough sit 2nd in the Championship table, despite failing to win either of their last two games.

#2197. Sabatini: "Como e Fabregas deludenti. Non giriamoci tanto attorno: Chivu è..."

Sabatini: "Como e Fabregas deludenti. Non giriamoci tanto attorno: Chivu è..."

Intervenuto su Youtube, Sandro Sabatini ha parlato così di Inter dopo la vittoria contro il Como a San Siro:

"Due parole sul Como, deludente. Anche Fabregas. D'accordo la stanchezza, il doppio impegno, un filo di presunzione, forse troppe feste per il 9-1 contro il Lens ma il Como ha deluso, aveva giocato meglio l'anno scorso contro l'Inter. Stavolta che è successo? Difficile dare le colpe al Como senza riconoscere i meriti dell'Inter.

Perché l'Inter, finalmente, dopo aver passato una decina di partite in cui si divertiva a concedere il 2-0 ad avversarie nettamente più deboli ha fatto tesoro di quello che è successo nel derby. Se l'Inter smette di partire con l'handicap 2-0, l'Inter non ha rivali in questo campionato. L'atteggiamento, la testa, l'impegno, la grinta, la determinazione, l'approccio di questa partita contro il Como non sono minimamente paragonabili a quelli del derby o in altre esibizioni precedenti

"Due parole su Chivu: passa un po' in secondo piano la sua importanza. Il Como di Fabregas, arriva Fabregas ma Chivu sta gestendo l'Inter anche con una capacità di tenere elevato il gioco, i risultati, gli umori dello spogliatoio, le pressioni, è da grande allenatore. Non giriamoci tanto attorno: Chivu è un grande allenatore e lo sta dimostrando di partita in partita e anche riscattando i mezzi passi falsi o i passi falsi come batoste come nel derby. Non è da tutti dopo un derby straperso presentarsi a Rotterdam, vincere 3-0, affrontare il Como e disintegrarlo con un punteggio che va ben oltre il 2-0, bravo Chivu"

#2196. Dimarco, giornata speciale: tagliato il traguardo delle 250 presenze con l'Inter

Dimarco, giornata speciale: tagliato il traguardo delle 250 presenze con l'Inter

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli FcInter1908 come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Un punto fermo, un'anima interista, un cuore nerazzurro. Un piede sinistro pericoloso come pochi altri al mondo, la capacità di alzare la testa e mettere il pallone dove vuole, che sia sulla testa di un compagno o all'incrocio dei pali. Federico Dimarco ha sognato e vissuto l'Inter da quando era bambino, crescendo con indosso una maglia nerazzurra e sperando di infiammare San Siro con i suoi gol e con le sue giocate. Un sogno diventato realtà per la prima volta l'11 dicembre 2014, il giorno del suo debutto in prima squadra, subentrando a Danilo D'Ambrosio nel secondo tempo di Qarabag-Inter. Il momento che Federico aspettava e desiderava, il primo passo di un percorso lungo, iniziato tra i primi calci nel Calvairate prima della chiamata nel Settore Giovanile nerazzurro.

Un percorso che si arricchisce di un nuovo, importantissimo traguardo nel match vinto 2-0 contro il Como, il numero 250 di Federico Dimarco con la maglia dell'Inter. Un cammino che lo ha visto crescere e diventare uomo, che lo ha visto superare difficoltà con lavoro e sacrifici. Ha atteso il suo momento, ha dimostrato il suo valore sul campo, è cresciuto partita dopo partita e stagione dopo stagione. Si è preso la fascia sinistra dell'Inter, e negli anni ha incantato il popolo nerazzurro, il suo popolo: cross al bacio, traccianti tesi da spingere in rete, traversoni arquati, tiri al volo, calci piazzati. Proprio con un calcio piazzato si è regalato il primo gol in nerazzurro: una punizione all'incrocio dei pali segnata a Marassi contro la Sampdoria il 12 settembre 2021.

La qualità sgorga dal suo mancino, il gioco pende dal suo lato, l'attacco nerazzurro nasce spesso dal suo estro. Si è eretto a punto fermo con costanza di rendimento, furore agonistico, voglia di vincere trofei con la sua Inter: 2 Scudetti, 3 Coppe Italia, 3 Supercoppe Italiane. Trofei su cui ha messo la firma con gol e giocate, con corse e ripiegamenti difensivi: dallo 0-3 nel derby valso la Supercoppa del 2022, aperto da una sua rete, alla cavalcata verso la Seconda Stella fino alla stagione del Double, in cui ha riscritto il record di assist nella storia della Serie A (18 nel campionato 2025/26): una stagione conclusa con il premio di Mvp della Serie A, il riconoscimento a un'annata dominante e storica. 250 volte con la maglia dell'Inter, con la sua maglia: il nuovo capitolo di una storia tutta nerazzurra.

2️⃣5️⃣0️⃣ presenze in nerazzurro e una vita passata con i nostri colori addosso 👏Complimenti, Federico Dimarco 🖤💙 pic.twitter.com/dqDqLqFn8n

#2195. Siparietto De Rossi-Modric: "Gli ho detto: 'Non ti posso menare perché non gioco più'"

Simpatico siparietto per Daniele De Rossi con Luka Modric, durante Genoa-Milan, sfida terminata 1-2 in favore dei rossoneri. Un ex campione diventato allenatore e un altro campione che in campo ci rimane ancora a 41 anni: il tecnico del Genoa ha confidato ciò che ha detto al trequartista croato durante la sfida.

Queste le sue parole: "Penso che sia emozionante vedere u giocatore di 41 anni continua a dipingere calcio così. L'anno scorso l'avevamo marcato a uomo a tutto campo con Vitinha. E' un piacere averlo rivisto, ci siamo beccati in campo due volte e gli ho detto: ’Non gioco più quindi non ti posso menare'. Lo stesso Rabiot, che è più giovane, sono giocatori mondiali".

Che margine di crescita può avere la squadra? "Il grande lavoro che ha fatto Diego quest'estate, nonostante non siamo riusciti a mettere qualità sugli esterni, ha creato una squadra forte. L'ho detto a Lecce, i giocatori miei sono più forti di quelli del Lecce e devo farli rendere al meglio io".

La sosta per recuperare tanti ragazzi? "La sosta sarà importante anche per questo. Faremo un allenamento congiunto a Chiavari e daremo minuti a questi uomini. A Bucarest vista da fuori sembrava una scampagnata ma è servita tanto".

#2194. Sheffield United man answers question over long-term future after West Ham, QPR summer interest

Sheffield United saw Harrison Burrows endure a tougher 2025/26 season after a fantastic debut campaign in 2024/25. Rival Championship clubs were lurking in the summer in case SUFC opted to cash in on the 24-year-old, with West Ham and QPR credited with interest while Burnley saw a late bid rejected.

However, Burrows has been back in the side and back in form in recent weeks. While speaking to The Star, the former Peterborough United man was asked about his long-term future and whether he sees it with the Blades, to which he responded: “Yeah, 100%. I’m really enjoying my football here. I really enjoy being with the lads, I really enjoy the club and I really enjoy the city. So, yeah, I’m very happy.”

Burrows is currently under contract at Bramall Lane until 2028. Sheffield United are looking to tie down a number of players to new deals, and the left-back is one of them alongside Sydie Peck, Femi Seriki and Michael Cooper.

As touched on before, Burrows endured a fantastic debut year at Sheffield United. After signing from Peterborough United, he stepped up to the Championship brilliantly as he recorded six goals and six assists in 48 games for a Blades side that made the play-off final.

Form proved harder to come by last term, and Sam McCallum emerged as the first choice at left-back before injury hit. He locked down his spot in Chris Wilder’s side early on this season too, performing well while Burrows was sidelined himself.

Harrison Burrows’ Sheffield United stats (all competitions)

However, injury has cast McCallum to the treatment room once again, and Burrows has made a fine return to the team. He has started the last five Championship matches, with only Saturday’s meeting at Charlton Athletic ending in defeat.

Burrows’ form has served as a reminder of what he offers on the left, with his technical ability making him a creative asset for SUFC. There are questions over the defensive side of his game at times, but the quality he offers with the ball can see him play a highly influential role when he’s in good form.

Aged 24, Burrows is already approaching 300 career games. He played 185 times for former club Peterborough United, and Saturday’s start against Charlton Athletic marked his 101st appearance for Sheffield United.

It means he has both an excellent amount of experience and plenty of room to keep developing. Burrows spent his youth in midfield and broke into first-team football in this position too, so he still has progress to make defensively.

With improvements in this area, SUFC will have a well-rounded left-back with strong experience and leadership abilities – as shown by his past captaincy at Peterborough United.

A challenging season and summer interest brought questions over his Bramall Lane future, but Burrows can be a valuable asset in the now and future if a new long-term deal is agreed.

#2190. Fiorentina e Juventus come due pugili che non vanno al tappeto: 2-2 spettacolare al Franchi

Si chiude con uno spettacolare pareggio per 2-2 tra Fiorentina e Juventus l’11ª giornata di Serie A. Viola avanti per due volte, prima in avvio con Pellegrino, poi nella ripresa con Njie e ripresa i entrambe le occasioni da Woltemade e Bremer.

Nella bolgia del Franchi, colorata da una spettacolare coreografia, i viola partono fortissimo e trovano il vantaggio già dopo quattro minuti con Mateo Pellegrino, bravissimo a sfruttare un corner perfetto battuto sul secondo palo da Gonçalves e a segnare con il colpo di testa specialità della casa del bomber argentino, il quale ha voluto celebrare il quinto gol stagionale facendo ‘la mitraglia’ alla Batistuta. La rete della Fiorentina, però, non scioglie gli uomini di Spalletti, che con il passare dei minuti prendono in mano il pallino del gioco. Al 21’ bel tentativo dalla distanza di Casal che dopo aver raccolto un cross di Woltemade, si aggiusta la palla con il petto e calcia molto bene, costringendo De Gea ad un grande intervento in tuffo. Il pareggio è nell’aria e arriva al 31’: Kolo Muani scappa a Viery sulla destra e trova al centro Woltemade, marcato malissimo da Dragusin che gli lascia tutto il tempo di infilare De Gea con il più facile dei tap in che vale il primo gol in Serie A per il tedesco. Juventus che pochi minuti dopo va vicinissima al sorpasso: si ripete l’asse Woltemade-Kolo Muani ma questa volta è il francese a concludere in porta trovando una grande risposta di De Gea; la sfera carambola sui piedi del portiere spagnolo e carambola al limite dell’area dove Koopmeiners calcia di prima intenzione trovando la deviazione decisiva della difesa viola.

Ripresa che vive sulla stessa falsa riga del primo tempo. La Fiorentina parte forte e torna subito in avanti al 54’ con il terzo gol consecutivo di Njie, che è il più rapido di tutti nel ribadire in rete un cross di Jimenez rimasto in area. La Juventus però non si scompone e al 66’ acciuffa di nuovo gli avversari. Nel corner battuto da Douglas Luiz, Bremer prende l’ascensore, salta in testa a Dragusin e batte De Gea uscito malissimo nell’occasione. Dragusin di testa che troverebbe la rete del 3-2 ma l’arbitro strozza in gola l’urlo del Franchi rilevando una spinta del centrale rumeno ai danni di Bremer. AIn pieno recupero doppia clamorosa chance: prima Kolo Muani si lascia stoppare da Fagioli in uno contro uno in area, sul ribaltamento di fronte Mastantuono va vicinissimo al gol vittoria ma Vicario dice di no.

#2188. Director confirms ‘the next step’ with talks over deal for West Ham man eyed

West Ham loaned out flop striker Niclas Fullkrug in the summer, and his remarkable form since rejoining Werder Bremen has been well documented. The 33-year-old has 12 goals in 11 games this season, earning a recall to Jurgen Klopp’s Germany squad and prompting swift talk over a permanent WHUFC exit.

Now, Werder Bremen sporting director Clemens Fritz has spoken publicly about the matter, telling Sky Sports Germany (as relayed by the Hamburger Morgenpost) that the plan is to hold formal talks with the Hammers over a permanent move for Fullkrug.

“The next step is to enter into dialogue with West Ham,” Fritz said. “We will, of course, hold talks about this. Ultimately, we also have to see what the financial package looks like.”

West Ham signed Fullkrug for a hefty fee worth up to £27m in 2024. He had been a prolific scorer in Germany, but there were eyebrows raised over the amount paid given that he was aged 31 at the time.

Those concerns were justified, as Fullkrug failed to make a positive impact in English football. Across all competitions, he managed three goals and two assists in 29 games for the Hammers, so his summer exit to Werder Bremen was a much-needed one.

The German has been a prolific scorer in his home country across his career, but few expected him to find the form of his life on the return to Werder Bremen. Fullkrug has 12 goals in 11 games for the club, with Saturday’s 1-0 loss to VfB Stuttgart marking the first Bundesliga game he has failed to score in since August 30th.

His call-up to Klopp’s Germany squad shows just how well he has been doing. Few WHUFC fans would have expected such success given the standard of his performances over two years in East London.

When players out on loan hit impressive form, it is often met with clamour for their return. However, that is highly unlikely to be the case for Fullkrug.

His circumstances are different. Not only did West Ham pay out a big transfer fee and sizeable wages, but he turns 34 later this season, so will likely have eyes on seeing out the twilight years of his career enjoying his football back in his home country.

Fullkrug’s stint at WHUFC is one best forgotten, and neither he nor the club should have a problem with bringing their affiliation to a permanent end. He can focus on maintaining his form and happily seeing out his career in Germany while the Hammers get his wages off the books for good.

#2187. Three goals from 1.09 xG: £20m star’s stunning form inspires Birmingham City’s rise to the top of the Championship

Birmingham City defeated fellow promotion hopefuls Swansea City on Saturday, making it six wins from the last seven to move to 1st in the Championship. BCFC have put in some fantastic team performances in this run, but as far as individual efforts go, it is hard to overlook that of Jay Stansfield.

The £20m Blues man has moved back to striker in August Priske‘s absence for the last two games, and he has netted three goals. In victories over Millwall and the Swans, Stansfield has over-performed his xG of 1.09 by 1.91 (FotMob), providing a timely reminder of his goalscoring threat at St. Andrew’s.

Playing as an attacking midfielder, Stansfield has been more of a facilitator than a goalscorer in recent times, with his well-rounded game and fantastic work rate seeing him retain a key role in Chris Davies’ side. Blues fans needed no reminder of his importance, but the 24-year-old’s return to the scoresheet in the last two games has affirmed just how good he is as the lead man up top.

Stansfield is one of the faces of Birmingham City’s rebuild. He impressed on loan despite relegation in the 2023/24 season, and was the statement signing of Tom Wagner and co ahead of their record-breaking League One title win, joining permanently for a reported fee of up to £20m.

23 goals in 44 games across the 2024/25 campaign helped inspire City back to the Championship, and he followed that up with 11 goals in all competitions last term. However, a move out of his natural striker role has seen goals decrease for Stansfield.

Before November started, Stansfield had just three league goals in 2026. As said before, his work rate in and out of possession and his technical ability in knitting attacks together meant he has held an influential role regardless – for the most part. However, three goals in four days have made the Tiverton-born forward pivotal in BCFC’s rise to the Championship summit.

Stansfield was everywhere against Swansea City. Not only did he score twice, but he created two chances and one big chance, took seven shots and covered plenty of ground (FotMob). His heatmap showed that, despite playing as the traditional striker, he was still dropping deep and drifting wide to pick up the ball and keep the Swans’ defence thinking.

It was the sort of performance that everyone knows Stansfield is capable of. He led by example in terms of both attribution and ability as Davies’ side moved top.

Birmingham City’s faith in Davies has paid off. After doubts over whether he could lead them to the next level, he has weathered the storm and turned his side into deserving Championship leaders.

The Welshman has a tough call to make amid Stansfield’s return to goalscoring form though. At striker, it feels like his entire skillset is being used as he finds more shooting opportunities.

Stansfield is more of a scoring threat at striker compared to attacking midfield. However, the form of Danish ace Priske at the top of the pitch cannot be ignored, and he is not capable of dropping into a deeper role like his attacking counterpart.

It is highly likely that Stansfield drops back into the no. 10 role when Priske is fit again. But, recent performances and historic stats suggest that Blues get their best out of their big-money star when he’s deployed at the top of the pitch.

It is a welcome dilemma for Davies to have, as Birmingham City enter the break on a high.

#2186. It pushes you forward – Jacob Murrell feeling fuelled by Falkirk fan fervour

Falkirk striker Jacob Murrell believes the passion of Scottish football is fuelling his goalscoring form.

The American has netted five goals since joining from DC United, helping the Bairns go on a four-game winning run ahead of Sunday’s trip to face Hearts.

The 22-year-old said: “It’s a little different just because the games matter a bit more here than they did back in the MLS, just because of the promotion/relegation and how much the fans care about results. So the intensity of the games is much higher, you’re not finding easy games.

“For me, it’s the best thing in the world to play under this type of pressure. It just gives me a purpose, it gives me that fuel.

“I love that aspect of it. I love that the way that our group goes on the field and performs matters to people. That’s the most special thing about what we get to do and it drives us on the hard days when we have to come to training, when we’re tired, when we’re sore.

Mika Baur identifies the Celtic challenge he is ready to rise to Neil McCann: Why nothing has changed in my approach to build strong Kilmarnock team

"We’re playing for something more than ourselves, that pushes you forward.

“Like after the Celtic match, when all the fans stayed in their seats, celebrated the win with us after, that wasn’t just a win for us as a team, but for the full club, for the fans, for the community. It was a really great moment for everybody.”

Murrell was among 17 summer signings but manager John McGlynn has quickly fashioned the group into an effective team.

“The people have been really good to me,” he said. “Falkirk in general has been really good to me. The club, the team-mates, but then the fans as well have been really good.

“There’s a bit of learning curve the first month to get up to speed and get all the stuff sorted with a car, an apartment and a bank account. All the stuff that you don’t see behind the scenes.

“Little stuff that’s different, like when I went to the grocery store, I couldn’t find eggs. Back home, they’re only in the fridge. They’re on the shelf here.

“But I’ve got into a really good rhythm with a couple of team-mates going to the gym after training and just getting into good habits where we’re cooking our own food and eating well.

“Just feeling full of confidence that we’re putting in the work and we’re going to reap the benefits of it.”

#2185. Why James Morrison and West Brom fans are tipping 26-year-old star for a Premier League rise as impeccable form continues

West Brom sit 5th in the table after a 3-0 win over Millwall on Saturday made it six games without defeat. Isaac Price and Jayson Molumby made the headlines as they both scored one and set up another, but it was a fantastic team performance from WBA who strengthen their promotion credentials with every passing match.

Although not a regular on the scoresheet, one man never far away from praise is Ousmane Diakite. And, once again, the Malian midfielder was excellent on Saturday, playing the full 90 minutes in midfield as the Baggies stormed to victory.

Focusing on the win over Millwall, Diakite was key in his usual role of winning duels, guarding the backline and keeping the ball moving. Primarily operating on the left of the midfield two, the West Brom man completed 51 of his 58 passes, five of which were into the final third while one created a chance.

In duels is where the 26-year-old found most of his success though, as has been the case all season. Diakite won seven duels in total: six on the ground and one in the air. He also completed three tackles, made two interceptions and four recoveries (FotMob).

This latest effort contributed to his eye-catching totals across the Championship campaign to date. No central midfielder has won more than his 92 duels, and he ranks amongst the league’s best in his position for aerial duels won (28 – top 2%), clearances (44 – top 2%) and defensive contributions (98 – top 3%).

On the ball, WBA star Diakite has completed 595 passes (top 9%), made 86 line-breaking passes (top 23%), made 13 successful dribbles (top 8%) and won 23 fouls (top 4%). As far as his stats go, he has been one of the Championship’s most effective midfielders of the season so far in all key areas apart from chance creation and goalscoring with those responsibilities falling to Albion stars Price, Jimmy-Jay Morgan, Aune Heggebo and more.

After a challenging start in a West Brom shirt, Diakite proved his worth in last season’s relegation fight under James Morrison and deservedly earned a new deal. He has got better and better in the 2026/27 campaign, and is a crucial part of an Albion side that looks ready for a promotion challenge.

In the build up to the Millwall game, Baggies boss Morrison was asked whether Diakite was a Premier League player in the making. Speaking to reporter Steve Tudgay, he said: “Yes he is. With us!”

Similarly, former West Brom man Paul Robinson recently told the Express and Star: “He has Premier League attributes, when you look at him”.

Not only do Diakite’s stats back this up, but so do the rave reviews of WBA supporters. Over the past week or so, numerous fans have shared their belief in his potential on X:

Ousmane Diakite WILL be a premier league footballer. Hopefully with us but time will tell #wba

Diakite walks into a bottom half Prem team all day for me #wba

Diakite could get into the Man united midfield all day, his improvement since joining has been unreal to watch #wba

West Brom fans will be hoping Diakite’s form continues. If it does, he may well get his deserved step up to the top flight at The Hawthorns.

#2184. Bournemouth blocked Birmingham City and Southampton deals - the tables have turned

Birmingham City and Southampton have emerged as two of the early-season promotion candidates in the Championship, with the goals certainly flowing at St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park and St Mary's respectively.

Chris Davies has addressed Birmingham's shortcomings from last season rather emphatically, whilst Saints haven't let their promotion ambitions be deterred by a four-point deduction they were served by the EFL.

Both managers have quality running right throughout their squads, and they have been particularly efficient in attack, with their sides scoring over 50 goals between them.

In the summer, the West Midlands and South Coast outfits were just two of the clubs heavily linked with a move for AFC Bournemouth attacker, Daniel Jebbison.

Jebbison is no stranger to the Championship, best-known for his time at Sheffield United, before moving to the Vitality Stadium.

However, based off early-season fortunes for the relevant parties, many will argue that Marco Rose has made the wrong call surrounding the 23-year-old's career.

The nine-time Canadian international's prior exploits have certainly garnered intrigue, as despite only scoring three times in 35 appearances at Bramall Lane, Jebbison sealed a £1.5m move to the Dorset club under Andoni Iraola.

Utilised as a bit-part player in 2024/25, the forward still made 34 appearances in all competitions, albeit many of those were as a substitute, before a loan move was sanctioned to Preston North End last summer.

Last season was one of extremely mixed fortunes for the Lilywhites and Jebbison, with the Oakville native netting six times in 38 appearances - the majority of which came in the first half of the campaign, before the form of Paul Heckingbottom's side dropped like a stone.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

Despite seeing Rose replace Iraola on the South Coast, it was largely expected that Jebbison would be allowed to leave on loan or permanently in the summer window, and plenty of Championship sides were linked.

Two of those came in the form of Birmingham and Saints, with Davies looking to reshuffle his attacking options following Kyogo Furuhashi's failed transfer to St. Andrew's, whilst Eckert searched for even more firepower beyond Cyle Larin.

Blackburn Rovers and Wrexham were also credited with interest in the striker, before it emerged three days prior to the Championship season kick-off that Enas Unal's sale from Bournemouth to Getafe presented Jebbison with the chance to stake a claim for regular first-team minutes.

It has been an intriguing start to life in English football for Rose, with Bournemouth recording mixed results in the Premier League, although they have made a flying start to their maiden UEFA Europa League campaign.

There were reservations over how the German's squad would cope with the swift turnarounds that come with such short-term success, and Jebbison, who also spent time on loan with Watford, claimed in September that he would be ready whenever needed.

"I'll get my chance and every minute I get, every second or whatever, I'll work my hardest so that's what I'm trying to do, just take it day-by-day," he said.

However, that is yet to be the case, as he still finds himself behind Evanilson, Alvaro Rodriguez and Junior Kroupi in the pecking order, which could see him potentially have one eye on a January exit.

#2183. Marink Reedijk lays down Aberdeen challenge to Hibs team after Hearts win

Hibernian manager Marink Reedijk has challenged his players to replicate their Hampden hunger against Aberdeen as they get back in the “rat race”.

“It was a very beautiful moment,” he said. “After the match, you saw what it meant to the fans, what it meant to the club and what it meant to the players. That is important because this is what football is about.

It pushes you forward – Jacob Murrell feeling fuelled by Falkirk fan fervour Mika Baur identifies the Celtic challenge he is ready to rise to

#2182. Birmingham City told to consider 'no-brainer' transfer reunion with £15m man

#2181. He played Huddersfield Town & Luton - Then he was jailed over charity fraud

He made over 500 senior club appearances and played against both England and Argentina in the World Cup finals, but when Efe Sodje was found to have been siphoning off money from a charity in his name, none of that mattered in the slightest.

With his distinctive do-rag headband, Efe Sodje was a familiar face English football over the first decades of this century. Sodje had the sort of career that most EFL players could only dream of. Not only did he enjoy a twenty-year career which took in eleven clubs, but he was also recognised at international level.

Born in London to Nigerian parents, Sodje was picked for the Nigeria international team twelve times even though he never kicked a ball in the Premier League, and this included being selected for their national team squad for the 2002 World Cup finals, where he played against two former winners of the competition, both England and Nigeria.

Sodje brought his playing career to a close in 2015 at 33 years of age, but less than two years later he found himself in court on fraud charges and, after his conviction on those charges, ended up spending time at her majesty's pleasure.

Born in London in October 1972, Efe Sodje made his breakthrough in the senior game in the Football Conference (now the National League) in 1994. After three years at Broadhall Way, he left for newly-promoted Macclesfield Town in 1997, starting a spell in the EFL which would last for 16 years.

Sodje would make 303 appearances in the League over this time, during which he played for eight League clubs, including Luton Town, Huddersfield Town, Crewe Alexandra and Southend United. His longest spells came at Crewe, for whom he made 113 appearances, and Bury, for whom he made 212.

He won honours at six different clubs. In the non-league game, he was part of the Stevenage team which won the Football Conference title in 1996, but it was in the Football League that he picked up most of his silverware. He was promoted from the third tier with Southend United in 2006 - and was part of their team which knocked Manchester United out of the League Cup in November 2006 - and was promoted from the fourth tier no fewer than four times, with Macclesfield Town in 1998, Crewe Alexandra in 2003, Huddersfield Town in 2004 and Bury in 2011.

But he was perhaps best known for his surprise appearances for the Nigeria international team. While with Luton Town during the 1999-2000 season, he was called up to the national team squad for the 2000 African Cup of Nations, in which his country finished as runners-up to Cameroon, although he didn't play in the tournament.

But the bigger shock came two years later, when he wasn't just called up to the squad for the 2002 World Cup finals, but played against both Argentina and England, although his team was knocked out in the group stages of the competition. In total, Sodje - whose brother Sam also played international football for Nigeria - made 12 appearances for his country.

Sodje ended his playing career in mysterious circumstances, though. He played his last two seasons back in the National League with Barrow, and then back at Macclesfield. But when his departure from the game was announced in June 2016, by which time he was 42 years old, no reason was given for it.

Early in 2019, Sky Sports ran a story which may have explained Sodje's sudden departure from professional football. In September 2017, he - along with two of his brothers, Bright and Stephen - had been given jail sentences for their involvement in defrauding a charity which had been set up in their name.

It was reported that the three had siphoned off £63,000 which had been donated to the Sodje Sports Foundation, a charity ostensibly set up to provide aid for poor children in the Niger delta. Sky reported that, "The three brothers used their connections within professional football and business communities to secure support for their foundation and to give it an appearance of legitimacy."

Efe Sodje was imprisoned for 18 months, Bright Sodje for 21 months, and Stephen Sodje for two years and six months. As well as this, Sam Sodje - who himself made over 200 senior appearances for an array of clubs, including Brentford, Leeds United and Charlton Athletic - was acquitted, while Sam Sodje's brother Akpo fled the UK during the investigation, leading to the issuance of a European Arrest Warrant after he refused to return from Dubai over the investigations. He had previously been connected to a spot-fixing case while playing for Tranmere Rovers.

When handing down the sentences in January 2019, Judge Michael Topolski QC told the defendants: "You have brought shame upon yourselves and your family." Having been given the huge leg-up of the sort of sporting ability that many people would give anything for, it's impossible to avoid the conclusion that the judge was entirely right in his assessment of them.

#2180. Celtic's 'Che Guevaras' should show their fellow fans some respect at this year's AGM

There is about as much chance of Dermot Desmond taking a seat amongst the Union Bears ultras group the next time Celtic play Rangers at Ibrox in January as there is of the billionaire Irish financier rocking up at their AGM at Parkhead next month.

Desmond, who has been the Glasgow club’s principal shareholder for more than two decades, has not attended the gathering since way back in 2006 and he is not, with disaffection about the stewardship of the Scottish champions still rife in the stands, about to break with tradition.

The greeting which chief executive Michael Nicholson, chief financial officer Chris McKay, interim chairman Brian Wilson and manager Martin O’Neill received from some protesters when they attended a question-and-answer session at the Greenock Supporters’ Club this week gave an indication of the sort of treatment he would receive if he did.

Speaking at his pre-match press conference ahead of the Europa League encounter with Besiktas on Thursday night, O’Neill expressed his dismay at the fact that around 25 supporters appeared wearing face coverings. “That was the saddest thing,” he said. “I would never have thought that fans would be clothed in balaclavas at a meeting.”

The frustration in the statement which the Celtic Fans Collective, an umbrella coalition of supporters’ clubs and organisations, published this week demanding that Desmond make a rare appearance at the meeting was palpable and understandable.

That the man who, rightly or wrongly but unquestionably, wields the most power at Parkhead is an annual no-show highlights everything that is wrong with how the former European Cup winners are being run. Are accountability and dialogue too much to ask for?

Is the famously publicity-shy man from Macroom in County Cork, though, really going to trap when the same individuals who hurled tennis balls at him when he was playing golf in the Alfred Dunhill Championship at St Andrews last month are likely to be in attendance?

There has been fevered speculation about when Celtic directors will face their shareholders in recent weeks. Supporters are desperate to know what their long-term football strategy is, who has been driving recruitment, how governance is going to be modernised, why tens of millions of pounds continue to sit in the bank and much else besides.

But are they likely to get answers to those questions now the AGM date has been set for Tuesday, December 1? The actions of a certain sub-section of their support in Inverclyde in midweek suggested that it could well be another bad-tempered, ugly and ultimately futile get-together.

Celtic interim chairman Brian Wilson, left, chief executive Michael Nicholson and chief financial officer Chris McKay at Parkhead (Image: Stuart Wallace / Shutterstock)

Ill-feeling about then-chairman Peter Lawwell bringing last year's annual meeting to a premature end amid persistent disorder still lingers. It has been suggested once again in recent days that the move was a cynical ploy by the besieged board to avoid public scrutiny.

However, David Low, the financial analyst who was a driving force behind the Fergus McCann takeover back in 1994 and is now associated with the Celtic Supporters Limited pressure group, rejected that interpretation of events when he appeared on the A Celtic State of Mind podcast last week.

“You are allowed to hold the people who shareholders elected to account once a year,” he said. “But it is a professional environment. Last year’s meeting was a bun fight. Nobody came out of that looking well. It is a professional forum and you have to respect the forum you are in.

“Give them red cards if you have to if they give an unsatisfactory answer. But listen to what is said and then react. That is the way it has always been at Celtic, even when it was a private company.

“That was a horrible meeting last year. People go there who want to learn. That is why they are there. You turn up, you ask questions and you try to learn something. If you don’t like it you can boo and do whatever within reason. But you have got to listen.

“It disintegrated into a shambles last year. It was over-the-top. There was a lot of unacceptable behaviour from the hardcore. It was not on. It was the biggest AGM in 20 years.”

There has been widespread unhappiness about the course Celtic are on for some time and a substantial section of the support wants meaningful change in how the Parkhead club is run. But some of the more vocal and militant members of the rebel alliance can be arrogant and selfish.

They did not merely show disrespect to the board last year. They showed disrespect to the hundreds of fellow shareholders who had turned up, many from far afield, wanting to exercise the same right to scrutinise those running their club.

The club later alleged that its head of safety, security and operations, Mark Hargreaves, had been threatened and intimidated by two prominent members of the Green Brigade. Whatever the truth of those allegations, it was a sorry affair from start to premature finish.

Celtic fans hold up banners referencing their teams failures against teams in Europe (Image: Andrew Milligan)

One lifelong fan voiced his exasperation at the leaders of the civil war that was raging at his beloved club in these pages as he attended the Scottish Gas Scottish Cup game against Dundee back in February.

“We don't need modern-day Che Guevaras,” he said before he ignored the boycott that had been called. “We want people who love Celtic.” He is not alone in taking that view.

The Celtic Fans Collective appealed for the “focus to be firmly on scrutiny rather than protest” at the AGM in their latest missive. They are right. The board should be made to answer searching questions. Those who shout directors down before their answers can be heard are serving neither their cause nor their fellow supporters.

It would perhaps help the Parkhead club to move forward on and off the park if those words were heeded and there were no disruptions from “aggressive and irrational factions” hellbent on “stirring up toxicity”.

#2179. Rangers and Portsmouth swerved transfer bullet as Frank Lampard hit the buffers

Portsmouth head into the international break on an excellent run of form, as John Mousinho's side dare to dream of a place in the Championship eight come May.

Portsmouth, after a difficult start to the campaign where they won just two of their opening eight league games, have bounced back superbly, and instead of looking like relegation candidates, look like an outside shout for a play-off place.

Head coach Mousinho has done a superb job of taking Pompey from the depths of League One and cementing their status in the Championship, as now, the 40-year-old is aiming to take them a level higher.

Recruitment, on the whole, has been exceptional at Fratton Park over recent years despite a lack of funds, as Portsmouth may well have dodged an expensive bullet courtesy of their lack of financial muscle.

Under the ownership of Eisner's, Portsmouth have never spent beyond their means; instead, they have recruited smartly by uncovering undervalued talent across the globe.

Nicolas Schmid joined from Blau Weiss Linz in Austria, Adrian Segecic arrived from Sydney FC in Australia, Rocco Shein from Fredrikstad in Norway, and Marko Milovanovic headed to Fratton Park from Spanish outfit Almeria.

Whilst this summer has seen Pompey splash the cash a bit more than what they have been used to, just six months ago was not a similar story, having lost out on the capture of Surinamese winger Jahnoah Markelo from Swiss outfit FC Zurich to Coventry City.

Mousinho revealed he was interested in signing the winger during the January window in April 2026, but cited his reasoning for not completing the deal as the hefty fee, as Markelo headed to then-division rivals Coventry for around £4.3 million instead.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top 25 most demanding fanbases in British football right now

Following his move to the CBS Arena, Markelo found himself competing for minutes alongside the likes of Romain Esse, Min-hyeok Yang, Tatsuhiro Sakamoto, and Ephron Mason-Clark, with Brandon Thomas-Asante also offering an option out wide.

Markelo came through the ranks of FC Twente before spending time with Go Ahead Eagles, NK Kustosija Zagreb, NK Celje, and FC Zurich before his move to Coventry, but what looked to be an exciting signing at the time quickly proved to be a confusing one.

The 23-year-old was only included in eight of 17 Championship matchday squads he was available for, playing just a total of 44 minutes across five substitute appearances for the Sky Blues, who would eventually earn promotion to the Premier League via the title, to little impact from Markelo.

Following Coventry's promotion, it was already apparent that Markelo did not have a big role to play under Lampard, and heading up to a division likely meant even fewer opportunities would be afforded to him, and so that proved to be.

Just six months after signing for Coventry for around £4.3 million, a permanent exit was on the cards for the winger, as Scottish giants Rangers reportedly opened talks with the Sky Blues over a deal for Markelo.

However, the Surinamese instead opted to head to the Middle East with United Arab Emirates-based outfit Shabab Al-Ahli, where he has further failed to set the world alight.

Markelo has made just eight appearances for the club to date across all competitions, failing to register a single goal contribution, as right now, it looks like Portsmouth and Rangers have dodged a bullet with this deal.

Do you think Portsmouth and Rangers have dodged a bullet by not signing Markelo, or would things have gone differently had he headed to either of those instead? Share your thoughts below!

With Markelo's lack of impact at either Coventry or Shabab Al-Ahli in the past 11 months or so, it's looking increasingly likely that both Portsmouth and Rangers have gotten away with one here.

Had Pompey had the funds to rival the Sky Blues for Markelo's signature back in January 2026, he may well have headed to Fratton Park instead, though whether he would have had better fortunes under Mousinho remains to be seen.

As it stands, the Surinamese has failed to justify his £4.3 million transfer fee, as Portsmouth and Rangers must both be relieved.

#2175. Manchester United player ratings: United failed to win despite advantage

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United and Tottenham Hotspur returned from the international break with plenty to prove after a disastrous start to the season.

United arrived with just one win, while Tottenham came to Old Trafford without a win.

Both teams had half-chances in the first half but little to show for them. Tottenham had the better chances with chances for Dominic Solanke, Connor Gallagher and Kudus, but none could take advantage of them.

Manchester United came close in the first half to score after Bryan Mbeumo raced towards the goal on a counter-attack to find Amad. He had a difficult shot, and eventually the shot was too weak to cause any damage. The first-half display was met by boos from the Old Trafford crowd at half-time and during the first half as well.

Solanke had another chance at the start of the second half but put it over the top, amid a wrestle with Lisandro Martinez in the six-yard box. It was United, however, who started the second half with promise.

Substitutions by Michael Carrick around the hour mark made the difference as United showed intent before Mbeumo got the goal off Cunha’s low cross. Luke Shaw was at the end of a scuffle with Bentancur, who was shown a red card for headbutting Shaw.

United failed to capitalize on their one-man advantage and seemed to sit on their 1-0 lead, but Solanke punished them with a header to level the game in injury time. The draw left United with just 6 points off their first six games. The result leaves United with their worst start in 40 years.

Lenne Sammens-6          Quiet afternoon for United no.1 till the goal, where he saw the ball go into the back of the net.

Leny Yoro-6       Defensively was fine but offered little upfront. Played out of position, and he showed good defensive awareness on the right back till his substitution.

Harry Maguire-5.5            Was decent in the first half but wasn’t much of an answer to Tottenham going forward in the second half

Lisandro Martinez-5      Took his eyes off the ball, resulting in conceding a late goal off Solanke.

Luke Shaw-6      Went up high on the pitch and remained a threat with his overlapping threat. Was involved in the scuffle that got Bentancur a red card for headbutting.

Kobbie Mainoo-6.5        Brilliant defensive tackle to deny Tottenham in the 68th minute. Remained composed in tight spaces against a physical Tottenham midfield.

Youri Tielemans-6          A decent performance and was solid throughout the minutes he was on the field

Bruno Fernandes-5        Another quiet and disappointing evening for the United captain after he failed to impress on a day he needed to get a result for United.

Amad-6              A positive return from injury, showed plenty of promise on the wing but failed to get past a resolute Tottenham side

Matheus Cunha-7           One of the liveliest options for United going forward. Provided an assist to Mbeumo to put United up front

Bryan Mbeumo-7.5        A brilliant finish off Matheus Cunha’s low ball to put United in front. Was dangerous going forward and denied a second on a counter by Kinsky off a brilliant long ball by Baleba.

Carlos Baleba (58’)-6.5  A decent debut overall for the Cameroon international. He put in a couple of dangerous balls up the field, including a clear ball to Mbeumo on a counter, who was denied by Kinsky.

Diogo Dalot (58’)-6      Provided a decent attacking threat when brought on around the hour mark.

Joshua Zirkzee (58’)-6   Showed plenty of promise and was a threat after he was brought in around the hour mark.

#2174. The Celtic 'rollercoaster' Mika Baur is getting used to & how he spends his days off

It is easy to forget that Mika Baur only turned 22 over the summer. The German midfielder displayed maturity beyond his years in Celtic's captivating Europa League victory over Besiktas on Thursday evening, chasing lost causes, contributing defensively, and progressing play forward when the opportunity arrived.

It was his finest performance since joining the club for an initial fee of £4.5 million from Paderborn in August.

Baur compared his first few months in Glasgow to riding a rollercoaster, with downtime away from the football pitch proving rare.

"There’s no time to think about it! We are playing every three days and I really try to fight and bring my heart on the field all the time," Baur said. "Sometimes it works, sometimes it doesn’t, but I hope people understand as a young player it can be tough to be comfortable in a new country, a new culture and new club.

"But I really try my best. I have felt good in my first few months but it is a bit of a rollercoaster.

"Playing in this unbelievable stadium in front of these fans, getting the call-up to the national team was a big honour. You feel the pressure with some of the games we have lost but I am getting used to it."

This is Baur's first time living away from home. He credits his partner for making the transition to Scotland smoother, even if chances to play some golf are limited.

"I’m grateful to my girlfriend as she does most things and makes it comfortable at home," Baur continued. "The club also have people who have helped us a lot to settle.

"But you don’t get much time – you get home and lie on the couch!

"It’s not all stressful though. It’s also nice to go somewhere new and learn the city, get to coffee shops and restaurants.

"We’ve been to the beach, we’ve been to Edinburgh, we try to see a lot on our day off.

"I also play golf – so I’ve come to the home of golf! I’ve only managed to get to the driving range though. When you are playing every three days it’s a bit much to play nine or 18 holes.

"My father and brother play so hopefully the weather will be okay around Christmas to maybe manage a few holes."

Thursday's win over Besiktas wasn't quite a hole-in-one, but it marked a pivotal forward step in Celtic's Europa League campaign.

Finally up and running after consecutive defeats to Ferencvaros, Benfica and Celta Vigo, Baur gave credit to a raucous home support.

"The fans helped a lot," he explained. "You could feel the energy. It was not just us players on the field who deserved the win – it was also everyone around the stadium and they brought the energy as well.

"It’s an unbelievable feeling as a player to have everyone pushing you and being there for you. It gives you that last percentage of energy you need at the end.

"There is always pressure to win here, to win every three days. That’s what we all want. In the last two games it wasn’t possible for us. We knew it wasn’t good enough but here we played well and deserved it."

"The win was important after some tough weeks," Baur continued. "But the belief and the way we work together as a team has always been good.

"There was never any problem with that because we always believed in ourselves. We showed how we can leave our hearts on the field and put in a shift.

"It was important to get those three points and we deserved it. Hopefully we can go forward with confidence from this game."

Despite his stellar showing on Thursday, Baur missed out on Jurgen Klopp's latest Germany squad. He made his senior international debut against Serbia last month as the former Liverpool manager had named two separate squads for four fixtures.

Klopp's group was reduced this time around, though Baur will return to the under-21s.

"I can understand," Baur said of the snub. "There are so many unbelievable players in the squad, especially in my position.

"We’re talking about guys from Bayern Munich and Borussia Dortmund. I just do my best and hopefully they can see I am developing and improving.

"I’m still with the under-21s for the games against Belgium and Croatia, so that will be good. European matches like Thursday can only help and I have to hope they see.

"I’m still young but I’ll keep doing my best and we’ll see what happens next time."

#2173. Michael Carrick reacts to Man Utd boos after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick admitted Manchester United’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur was a painful result, after his side failed to protect the lead against 10-man Tottenham at Old Trafford.

Manchester United looked set to claim three points when Bryan Mbeumo scored in the second half off Cunha’s low cross, but Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser in injury time left Carrick facing more questions on United’s performance and his future.

Speaking to BBC Match of the Day, the United manager felt his players improved after the break but failed to handle the situation when victory was within reach.

“It was a mixed game. We found it tough to start with. For a number of reasons, we were not quite there. You could see it was a challenging time,” Carrick said.

“But the lads grew into the game, in the second half I thought we were better in everything. To get the game as we wanted it, in such a good position – we should see it through. We have to manage those situations.”

United have now conceded two late goals that have cost them valuable points, something Carrick believes has made their position look worse than it might have otherwise.

Asked whether a lack of confidence had contributed to United’s slow start, he disagreed but admitted matches can quickly slip away from his side.

The boos at half-time and full-time also clearly affected him.

“It’s not nice, it hurts a lot because I care about this club. But I understand the disappointment, the reality is what it is, we need to get back to winning games.”

#2172. Melvine Malard becomes Chelsea’s leading scorer as FOUR-goal start strengthens her claim to the No.9 role

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Melvine Malard’s Chelsea goals are beginning to provide a persuasive answer to the club’s centre-forward question.

Her opener in Sunday’s 3-0 victory over West Ham took the summer signing to four competitive goals, making her Chelsea’s leading scorer this season.

That total comprises two goals in Champions League qualifying and two in the Women’s Super League.

The circumstances make those contributions particularly useful. Across three scoring appearances, Malard has broken a deadlock, restored a lead and helped Chelsea put difficult matches beyond their opponents.

Three of those goals arrived while Chelsea were level or protecting a one-goal advantage.

That gives the early return significance beyond its size. Malard has repeatedly contributed while there was still a contest to resolve.

Sunday provided the latest example. Chelsea had plenty of possession but needed somebody to turn an opening into a lead.

Turn and finish 💥Melvine Malard finishes for @ChelseaFCW 🔵#BarclaysWSL pic.twitter.com/eicBNrYMYk

According to Chelsea’s official match report, the Blues reached the half-hour mark without a shot on target despite having 64 per cent possession.

Sjoeke Nüsken created the breakthrough by winning possession and finding Malard.

The striker received the ball with her back to goal, held her position, turned and finished into the bottom corner.

It was an important demonstration of what she can offer centrally. Chelsea could find her under pressure and still get a shot away, rather than needing to release her into open space.

Nüsken’s contribution deserves equal recognition. Her pressing created the opportunity that Malard converted.

The sequence offers Bompastor something useful to build on: winning possession higher up the pitch and finding a forward capable of finishing before the opposition recover.

Malard’s first three Chelsea goals came as a substitute.

Her two-goal competitive debut against Real Sociedad immediately demonstrated that capacity to change a match.

Nüsken released her for the goal that restored Chelsea’s lead at 3-2. Ellie Carpenter subsequently supplied her second, extending the advantage to three goals.

The official account of that European victory records two different routes to goal: a run beyond the defence and a finish from a delivery across the box.

Against Birmingham, her alertness produced another valuable contribution. Malard punished hesitation over a backpass to score Chelsea’s second, eight minutes after coming on, as detailed in the club’s match report.

Those appearances give Bompastor flexibility. Malard has already influenced matches when introduced against tiring opponents, while West Ham supplied a scoring return from the starting role.

Chelsea signed Malard from Manchester United on a four-year contract, bringing her back together with a coach who first identified her potential at 14.

Bompastor later coached her in Lyon’s senior side. Their relationship therefore predates this summer’s recruitment by years.

Speaking when Chelsea announced her signing, Malard said:

“My objective is to become better every single day and to win trophies.”

Her next task is sustaining this contribution across a longer run of matches.

Competition remains productive. Aggie Beever-Jones replaced her at West Ham and supplied Maika Hamano’s late goal, giving Bompastor another reason to value both forwards.

Malard’s strongest argument is the range of situations she has already influenced. She has attacked space, finished crosses, punished hesitation and scored with a defender close behind her.

Four goals offer an encouraging early return. The variety behind them gives Chelsea something substantial to develop.

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

#2171. Bryan Mbeumo reacts to Man Utd draw with Tottenham

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bryan Mbeumo admitted Manchester United have work to do after their frustrating 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, despite scoring a superb second-half goal at Old Trafford.

The Cameroon international put Manchester United ahead in the 73rd minute, finishing in the far corner after Matheus Cunha created the opportunity from a low cross. However, Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory, even though Tottenham had been reduced to 10 men following Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card.

Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2).

3 – Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2). Disadvantage? pic.twitter.com/DuSpvymZfu

Speaking to Sky Sports after the game, Mbeumo acknowledged that United had taken too long to find their rhythm.

“We took time to get into the game, but we played well in the second half. In the first half, that wasn’t us, we had to change. We tried to get at them,” he said.

Mbeumo recognised the threat Tottenham posed and admitted the result was disappointing, particularly given United’s position in the game before Solanke’s late goal.

“We know Spurs have good players who can be dangerous. It’s a bit disappointing, we need to work on things.”

United have struggled for consistency in the opening weeks of the season, and dropping points after taking the lead will do little to ease the pressure on Carrick. Mbeumo, however, insisted the team must look ahead rather than dwell on the setback.

“We need to keep going forward, as we have a lot of games.”

Michael Carrick was pleased with Mbeumo’s contribution, highlighting both the quality of his finish and the different roles he has played since joining United.

“Bryan’s done great. It was a fantastic finish again; he’s obviously played different roles for us but we know what he offers,” the United manager said.

“He’s got good quality and obviously the more goals he scores, the better. He’s doing really well for us.”

Mbeumo’s goal was one of the few bright moments in another difficult afternoon for United. Carrick’s team must now find a way to turn things around from here after just six points from six games.
